2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消

の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み
―2027年にかけてフランス大統領選やスペイン・イタリアの総選挙など、欧州主要国で重要な国政選挙が相次ぐ。極右・ポ

Document 076: 2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/5 
2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 
～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 連合は2027 年春闘基本構想で、 定昇込みの賃上げ要求を 「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベア等の
賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とする目安を示した。前年よりも要求姿勢をやや強めて
おり、５％台の賃上げの定着に加え、さらなる引き上げを目指す姿勢がうかがえる。 
○ 経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経労委報告の骨子案では賃上げ
モメンタムの「浸透・定着」が掲げられ、高い賃上げの継続に加え、中小企業への波及を重
視する姿勢が示されている。 
○ 日本経済研究センターのＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査によると、民間エコノミストは 2027 年
春闘の賃上げ率を4.96％と予想（厚生労働省ベース）。前年から小幅に鈍化するものの、高
い伸びが続く見通しであり、４年連続で５％台の賃上げを実現できるかが焦点となる。 
○ 春先にはイラン情勢悪化による賃上げ鈍化が懸念されていたが、現時点で労使双方の姿勢に
トーンダウンはみられない。企業業績の底堅さや構造的な人手不足が高い賃上げを支えてお
り、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは良好と評価できる。

連合は、「６％中心、５％以上」と昨年からトーンをやや強める 
2027 年春闘の集中回答日は2027 年の３月中旬だが、実際の交渉に向けての動きは既に始まりつ
つある。 
本日、連合は「2027 春季生活闘争基本構想」を公表した。春闘に向けて最初に注目される重要
イベントであり、翌年の春闘に向けた労働組合側の基本理念や方針、重点課題を示すものであ
る。この基本構想をみることで、労働側が翌年の春闘にどの程度強気な姿勢で臨もうとしている
かを早い段階で確認することができ、春闘全体のトーンをつかむうえで重要な材料となる。 
今回の基本構想の最大の注目点は、定期昇給相当分を含めて「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベースア
ップ等の賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、より高い賃上げを目指す姿勢を示したことで
ある。「５％以上」という要求水準自体は４年連続（2023 年は「５％程度」）となるが、今回は
「６％中心」との表現が新たに加わった。ベア等の賃上げ分についても、従来の「３％以上」に

### Page 2

2/5 
「４％中心」が加えられている。前年の2026 年春闘では、全体で「５％以上」 、中小で「６％以
上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者では「７％」を目安とする、いわゆる「５・６・７」の要求が
示されていた。今回、全体についても６％を明確に意識する形となったことは、実質的には５％
を最低ラインとしつつ、より高い賃上げを促す姿勢を強めたものと受け取れる。単に高い賃上げ
を続けるだけでなく、その水準をさらに引き上げようとする意欲がうかがえる。

こうした姿勢の背景にあるのが、ここ数年の高い賃上げ実績である。2026 年春闘では連合ベー
スの賃上げ率の実績が5.01％となり、３年連続で５％台の高い賃上げが実現した。５％という水
準がかつてほど実現困難な目標ではなくなりつつあるなか、連合としても前年と同じ要求目安を
繰り返すだけでは、さらなる賃上げを促すメッセージとして十分ではないと判断した可能性があ
る。５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持するだけでなく、６％近辺を意識して賃上げ率のさらなる引
き上げを目指す段階に入ったというメッセージと位置付けられる。 
賃上げを取り巻く環境も、こうした強気の姿勢を支えている。構造的な人手不足が続くなか、
企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き重要な経営課題であり、賃金引き上げの必要性は高
い。また、物価上昇率は2026 年度後半にかけて再び高まるとみられ、家計の生活コスト負担は重
い。名目賃金が増加しても、物価上昇に追いつかなければ生活水準の改善にはつながらない。実
質賃金を安定的にプラスにするためにも、労働側が高い賃上げを求める理由は十分にある。 
加えて、賃上げの広がりという点でも課題は残る。2026 年春闘では中小組合の賃上げ率が
4.69％と高い伸びを確保したものの、全体の5.01％には届かなかった。大企業と中小企業の間に
はなお差があり、正規雇用と非正規雇用の賃金格差も残っている。連合にとっては、高い賃上げ
春闘年 連合の要求目安（定昇相当分込み） 連合ベース 厚労省ベース

連合の賃上げ要求目安と春闘賃上げ率
実績

### Page 3

3/5 
を実現するだけでなく、それを幅広い企業や労働者に波及させることも引き続き重要な課題であ
る。今回の基本構想でも、賃金実態を把握できないなどの事情がある中小組合については
「20,000 円以上・６％以上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者については「７％」を目安とする方針
が示された。こうした点を踏まえれば、今回の基本構想は、高い賃上げの持続と一段の引き上
げ、そして賃上げの裾野の拡大を目指すものと評価できる。 
また、今回の基本構想では、新たに2030 年の賃金水準を2026 年比で15％程度引き上げる中期
目標も設定された。毎年の賃上げ率だけでなく、中期的に目指す賃金水準を明示することで、高
い賃上げを一過性のものに終わらせず、継続させようとする姿勢がうかがえる。 
なお、近年は個別組合の実際の賃上げ要求率がすでに６％近くに達しており、 「６％中心」とい
う今回の表現には、こうした実態を追認する意味合いもある。今回の基本構想からは、連合の強
い賃上げ意欲が確認できる一方、実際の賃上げ率が前年を上回るかどうかはまた別問題である。
今後は、主要産業別労働組合組織や個別組合の要求水準が前年からどの程度引き上げられるか
（られないか）が注目される。

経営側も賃上げの継続に前向き 
経営側も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経団連が毎年１月に公表する「経
営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労委報告） 」は、春闘に向けた経営側の基本スタンスを示すもので
あり、賃上げ交渉の方向性を占う上で重要な資料となる。2027 年春闘に向けた経労委報告につい
ては、現時点で原案は明らかになっていないものの、９月上旬には骨子案の内容が報道されてい
る。それによると、賃上げモメンタムの「浸透・定着」を掲げ、高い賃上げの継続と中小企業へ
の波及を促す方針とされる。 
経団連は2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年を「さらな
る定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の「浸透・定着」という表現からは、高い賃上げを継続す
るとともに、その動きを中小企業などに広げることを重視する姿勢がうかがえる。また、経団連
の筒井会長は９月７日の記者会見で、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付
けていきたい」と述べ、賃上げのモメンタムを維持する考えを示している。 
数か月前には、イラン情勢の悪化による原材料費や物流費の上昇が企業収益を圧迫し、2027 年
春闘では経営側が賃上げに慎重な姿勢を強めることも懸念されていた。しかし、現時点で経営側
の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられない。 
なお、経労委報告では、原案から正式な報告にかけての修正は例年あまり見られないが、骨子
案から原案にかけては内容が多少修正されることもある。今後、10 月末～11 月中頃までに報道さ
れるとみられる経労委報告の原案が明らかになれば、経営側のスタンスをより具体的に判断でき
るだろう。

エコノミスト予想は4.96％、５％を巡る攻防か 
10 月14 日に日本経済研究センターが公表した「ＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査」では、民間エコノ

### Page 4

4/5 
ミスト 34 人を対象に2027 年春闘の賃上げ率について特別調査を実施している。これによると、
予測値の平均は4.96％となり、2026 年実績の5.18％を 0.22％ポイント下回る見通しとなった。
もっとも、前年から小幅に鈍化するとはいえ、ほぼ５％という歴史的に高い水準であり、2027 年
も高い賃上げが続くとの見方が中心である。なお、ここでの賃上げ率は厚生労働省「民間主要企
業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」ベースであり、連合の集計とは対象が異なる。 
予測の内訳をみると、定期昇給分が1.69％、ベースアップ分が3.27％となっており、ベアにつ
いても３％を超える伸びが見込まれている。また、予測値の高位８機関平均は5.23％、低位８機
関平均は4.68％となっている。予測には一定の幅があるものの、高い賃上げが続くという点では
概ね認識は共有されている。

2027 年春闘で賃上げ率が前年からやや鈍化すると予想される背景には、2026 年の物価上昇率が
2025 年より低く、物価高に対応するための賃上げ圧力が幾分弱まることがあると考えられる。ま
た、数年にわたる高い賃上げによって人件費負担が累積していることに加え、イラン情勢悪化を
背景とした原材料費や物流費の上昇も、企業の賃上げ余力を抑制する要因となり得る。一方、企
業業績が足元まで好調を維持していることや、構造的な人手不足と採用競争の激化は引き続き賃
上げを促す要因であり、賃上げ率が大きく鈍化する可能性も低いとみられる。 
ちなみに、昨年の同時期のＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査でも、2026 年春闘の賃上げ率は4.81％
（2025 年10 月調査）と、前年実績からの鈍化が予想されていた。しかし、実際には5.18％と予
想を上回り、３年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現した。今回も前年からの小幅な鈍化が見込まれて
いるが、予測平均は昨年の同時期をやや上回っている。イラン情勢の悪化という新たな懸念材料
があるなかでも、エコノミストの間では高い賃上げが続くとの見方が維持されていることが分か

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
る。 
こうしたなか、予測平均の4.96％について、前年からの「鈍化」を強調するか、5％近傍という
「高水準の持続」を重視するかは、評価が分かれるところだろう。いずれにせよ、2027 年春闘で
は、３年連続で実現した５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持できるか、５％を巡る攻防が一つの焦点
となる。今後、経労委報告原案の判明により経営側の方針が明らかになるほか、主要産業別労働
組合組織の要求方針などが明らかになるにつれて、賃上げ率の見通しもより具体化していくこと
になるだろう。 
なお、この調査とは別に、毎年11 月～12 月にかけて、民間シンクタンク・調査機関から翌年の
春闘賃上げ率の予測が出されることが多い （筆者は 11 月に発表予定） 。 予測をレポートとして作成
することもあれば、 経済見通しレポートのなかで言及されることもあり、春闘賃上げ率の見通しに
ついて、この時期に市場の相場観が徐々に形成されていく。

2027 年春闘の初動は良好 
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の「初動のモメンタム」は、現時点では良好と評価できる。イ
ラン情勢が悪化した数か月前には、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益の圧迫を通じて、
2027 年春闘の賃上げ率が大きく鈍化するとの懸念もあった。しかし、ここまでの動きをみる限
り、労働側、経営側のいずれについても、前年と比べて賃上げ姿勢が慎重化している様子はみら
れない。連合が定昇込み、ベアともに前年より強い要求目安を打ち出し、さらに高い賃上げを志
向する一方、経団連も高い賃上げの継続と中小企業への波及に前向きな姿勢を示している。 
その背景には、原油などの代替調達が進み、資源の調達難が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が
回避されたことや、ＡＩ需要の拡大にも支えられ、企業業績が総じて堅調さを維持していること
があるとみられる。また、構造的な人手不足や採用競争の激化によって、企業が人材確保・定着
のために賃金を引き上げる必要性は依然として高い。物価上昇が続くなか、実質賃金の改善を求
める声が強いことも、高い賃上げに向けての動きを後押ししているとみられる。 
もっとも、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益への悪影響が今後さらに広がる可能性はあ
り、先行きを楽観視することはできない。また、現時点で確認できるのは労使双方の基本的な姿
勢であり、具体的な要求や回答の水準はまだ明らかになっていない。今後は、経団連「経労委報
告」の原案や主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針、早期に賃上げを表明する企業の動向などを通
じて、今回確認された前向きな姿勢が実際の賃上げにつながるかを見極める必要がある。

Industry View In-Line
At the rack level, Maia 300 is the key incremental story, while
Nvidia/AMD exhibits offered limited new system-level hardware.
Nvidia’s MGX component upgrades are the main supply-chain
watch points, although PTFE and next-gen interconnect and
cooling connector (ZQD) adoption remains uncertain.

Nvidia/AMD –  focus on ramps rather than new platforms. Most ODM displays
were similar to Computex Taipei (early June), so timing of ramp is key. For Nvidia,
VR200 racks have started a small-volume ramp, with the Nvidia Groq 3 LPX rack
following in late 2026/early 2027 and standalone Vera CPU racks in late 1Q27/early
2Q27, based on our checks. For AMD, Helios remained largely unchanged with
recent checks, with racks ramping in 1Q27, with pilot-build observations pointing to

Shennan Circuits for EAM PCBs and Fositek/Parker for quick disconnects.
Maia 300 -  a larger in-house inference opportunity. Microsoft plans production
deployments in 2027 as it scales Maia toward multi-gigawatt deployments. Each
rack integrates 96 accelerators within 167 kW. Based on that, each gigawatt of
deployment would imply 5 to 5.5K racks, and we believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could
be the ODM partners, while our pilot-build observations point to ISU Petasys for the

accelerator PCB, AVC for fans, and Fositek/Staubli for quick disconnects.
PTFE - technical upside, but no confirmed timing of adoption. Nvidia is evaluating
PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon following the Kyber delay and showcased its
NVLink PTFE PCB with seven suppliers (though we think there are suppliers missing
from this list). Better signal performance and reduced reliance on Q glass are
potential benefits, but processing complexity, higher costs and yield/qualification

risks remain hurdles to commercial adoption.
Ultra Dense Interconnect - next-generation interconnect opportunity for FIT.
Nvidia showcased six suppliers for this next-generation interconnect, which is
intended to replace Amphenol’s current HD2 connector in midplane and cable
cartridge applications. However, we believe adoption is still about two years away. In
the meantime, the supply chain is working to improve yields and bring ASPs below
US$100, with pogo-pin supply currently a bottleneck. Our checks identify FIT as the

key design partner for Kyber’s planned board-to-board connector, a position that
bodes well for its supply share once the product ramps.
ZQD - higher flow density and an expected ASP premium. Nvidia’s new ZQD
targets higher flow rates per unit area and was displayed with 14 suppliers.
Although the supply chain is working to improve yields and lower costs, greater
design complexity should keep ASPs above current QD pricing of US$20–30.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

2
Major Takeaways from OCP Summit 2026
ODM/OEM: Almost all the ODM/OEMs had a booth at OCP, but during our visit, we feel
most of the products were largely similar to what was showcased at Computex, such as
the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, the LPX tray, the Vera standalone tray. So net, we think from a
hardware perspective, there was nothing new on Nvidia or AMD. We believe the Nvidia
VR200 rack is already starting ramp in small volume, with the LPX rack starting to ramp

later this year or early next year, and the Vera standalone CPU rack ramping late 1Q27 or
early 2Q27.
AMD: Wiwynn, Meta, and AMD each showcased the Helios rack, which was unchanged
from last year’s OCP display with one exception: this year’s version featured the actual
compute tray in place of last year’s dummy tray, allowing a closer look. That said, the
compute tray itself was not new to us, as we had already seen it at OCP Taiwan in August.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Shennan Circuits is one

of the pilot build PCB suppliers for the MI455 Helios EAM PCB.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek (MQD02) and
Parker (UQD04) are one of the pilot build quick disconnect connector suppliers for
the MI455 Helios compute tray.
• HPE displayed the Helios switch tray at the OCP Summit. We believe the other
supplier of the Helios switch trays is Celestica.
Microsoft: Maia 300 represents a major expansion of Microsoft’s in-house inference

infrastructure. Announced on October 12, 2026, with production deployments planned for
2027, it forms part of Microsoft’s commitment to scale the Maia family to multi-gigawatt
deployments and make it the primary inference platform for its products and services. The
design targets long-context reasoning, conversational AI, and multi-step agentic
workloads, with an emphasis on improving inference economics through coordinated
silicon, software, and system design.

Maia 300 rack integrates 96 accelerators within a 167 kW power envelope, providing
approximately 27.6 TB of aggregate HBM capacity. There are 4 accelerators per tray,
implying 24 compute trays per rack, including six high-radix switch ASICs that are
integrated into three liquid-cooled switch trays, providing the rack-local connectivity
required by all 96 accelerators. Microsoft projects 40% more tokens per megawatt than
leading GPU-based systems and up to 7× Maia 200’s rack-level token throughput, though

this takes into account that the Maia 300 rack  includes twice as many accelerators per
rack.
• The Official Microsoft Blog A two-tier Ethernet scale-up network connects up to
128 racks, or 12,288 accelerators, while liquid cooling removes more than 80% of
rack heat. Each compute tray includes 8x fans  (Exhibit 3 ), and based on our
observations at the OCP Summit, we believe AVC is one of the pilot build fan
suppliers for the sample compute tray.

• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe ISU Petasys is one of
the pilot build PCB suppliers for Microsoft’s Maia 300 AI accelerator.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek and Staubli are
pilot build MQD03 (quick disconnect connector) suppliers for the compute tray.

Exhibit 3: Maia 300 server compute tray
Source: Microsoft.
Nvidia MGX Ecosystem
PTFE: We previously wrote that our supply chain checks suggest that Nvidia had initially
planned to adopt PTFE CCL for the very high-layer-count midplane in its Kyber
architecture. With Kyber now delayed, and Rubin Ultra no longer expected to use that
architecture, as previously discussed, the focus has shifted back to the Oberon
architecture. Nvidia is again evaluating PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon, and at this year’s

OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE PCB, with a total of 7 suppliers, including
VGT, TTM, Taiflex, SYTECH, Shennan Circuits, Kinwong, and Flexium (Exhibit 4  & Exhibit
5 ). However, our checks indicate that actual adoption for Rubin Ultra remains uncertain.
However, we find the supplier list puzzling. Our checks suggest that WUS PCB and
Unimicron are also working on this solution, and that Flexium, which has collaborated
with other PCB suppliers, may handle only a few process steps. Our checks also indicate

that Shennan Circuits, WUS PCB and VGT are now the leading PCB makers for this
solution, with relatively higher yields.
Advantages of PTFE
• Best signal performance: the lowest Dk and Df of the mainstream options, below
even standard M9-class CCLs. This matters most where insertion-loss budgets are
tightest, i.e. the highest speeds and highest layer counts.
• Stable electricals: the low Dk/Df hold up across frequency and temperature, and
PTFE absorbs very little moisture, so performance is stable in use.

• Less dependence on Q glass: because PTFE’s intrinsic dielectric constant is already
very low, reaching the target Dk requires less (and in some hybrid constructions,
none) of the expensive, supply-constrained Q glass. That is both a cost lever and a
supply-security benefit.
Disadvantages of PTFE

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
• Mechanically soft: low modulus makes it difficult to handle and to hold to
tolerance.
• Difficult lamination: PTFE does not flow like a thermoset, so bonding needs either
very high temperatures or lower-melting fluoropolymer (such as PFA
(polyfluoroalkoxy))/thermoset bond layers; its high, non-linear CTE stresses
copper adhesion and complicates layer-to-layer registration, and pressing typically
requires vacuum or an inert atmosphere with longer cycles.

• Difficult drilling: the soft, gummy resin tends to smear and can tear, and any
ceramic filler is abrasive on tooling. Also, the hole wall will not accept electroless
copper without an added activation step (sodium etch or plasma) on top of normal
desmear.
• Higher cost and qualification risk: materials and fabrication are both more
expensive, the qualified supply base is narrower, and yields at very high layer
counts carry risk.

Ultra Dense Interconnect: Currently, Nvidia's NVL72 racks are using Amphenol's Paladin
HD2 connectors for VR200 NVL72 midplanes and cable cartridges. Our previous checks
done around Computex 2026 in June, indicate ASP could reach US$70-90+ per set. For
the next-generation Kyber racks, our checks have previously indicated that a new midplane
connector called "Voronoi" will be introduced, and FIT is the key design partner for this

new board-to-board connector. At this year’s OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased a new
connector it calls the “Ultra Dense Interconnect”, with six suppliers in total. This was our
first in-person look at the design. We believe adoption is still two years out, and the
supply chain is currently focused on improving yields and optimizing costs to bring the
ASP below US$100. Our checks suggest that pogo pin supply is currently a bottleneck for
this connector.

Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Exhibit 6: Nvidia next-generation Ultra Dense Interconnect
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
ZQD: At COMPUTEX in Taipei earlier this year, we noted that our checks indicated Nvidia
was planning to introduce a new quick disconnect (QD) design for next-generation server
racks to achieve higher flow rates per unit area as cooling requirements rise. At this year’s
OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased that design, called ZQD, with 14 suppliers in total. Our

checks indicate the supply chain is also working to improve yields and optimize costs to
bring ZQD ASPs down. However, given the greater complexity of the design, we expect
ZQD ASPs to remain above current QD pricing of US$20–30 per set.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

M
Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 GM Dragged by Inventory
Write-off
Despite the secular tailwinds of increasing power consumption
in AI clusters, we think near-term sentiment toward Delta could
remain lackluster post weaker 3Q26 results.
3Q26 earnings fell short on lower GM:
• Off revenue of NT$197,059mn (+8% QoQ/+31% YoY), GM contracted  by
1.6ppt QoQ to 34.1% (vs. MSe/consensus at 35.7%/36.2%), which was
dragged 2ppt lower by the inventory write-off

• Opex was well controlled at NT$34,535mn (opex ratio 17.5%), lower than our
estimate of NT$36,983mn (opex ratio 18.4%) and consensus of NT$37,129mn
(opex ratio 18.0%)
• Operating profit came in at NT$32,620mn (+7% QoQ/+31% YoY; OPM
16.6%), 7% lower  than MSe of NT$34,992mn (OPM 17.4%) and 13% lower
than consensus of NT$37,677mn (OPM 18.2%).
• With non-op gains of NT$1,046mn (including FX gain of NT$415mn), net
profit was NT$23,495mn (-7% QoQ/+26% YoY; EPS NT$9.04), 8%/15% short

of MSe/consensus at NT$25,554mn (EPS NT$9.84)/NT$27,611mn (EPS NT
$10.40).
Our view:
• We believe near-term sentiment toward the stock will stay soft, though the
weak financial performance in 3Q26 was mainly impacted by component
supply tightness
• We believe Delta should grow its revenue by 10% QoQ with margin
expansion in 4Q26, and will continue to benefit from the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters in 2027
• Earnings call at 3pm HKT on Oct 29th  (details here)

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

M
Idea
VSMC could cause  high-single digit (%) dilution to 2027 GM – above many
investors’ 3-5% expectation: On Vanguard’s latest earnings call, management noted
a successful trial run of VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Company, its JV
with NXP Semiconductors. It confirmed that interposers are on track for mass
production  in 1Q27. We see revenue upside from the ramp – but depreciation could
heavily pressure margins. Management previously guided that VSMC could break

even at 30kwpm, more likely in late 2028, in our view (vs. current 8-inch margin at
30-35%). VSMC  capacity is scheduled at 44kwpm, ~100kwpm 8-inch equivalent,
about one-third of current 8-inch capacity (280kwpm). On the other hand, we think
new capacity may only run at <5kwpm in 1Q27 and <20kwpm by end-2027;
depreciation reflects 44kwpm (VSMC's full capacity) for infrastructure, clean room,
and construction. We therefore project that VSMC's gross margin dilution in full-

year 2027 could be more severe than many expect.
Despite strong customer commitment, VSMC could still dilute the corporate
margin average for at least the next five years: Since management previously
guided that VSMC could break even at 30kwpm, we view its break-even point as late
2028 or 1H29. It will then take years for GM to rise from 0% to 30-40%, even if the
capacity is fully booked. We thus expect a drag on corporate average GM for at least

the next five years. The good news: VSMC demand is skewed to high-margin
businesses –  interposers, industrial, and automotive – so the margin improvement
could be smoother  vs. peers like Hua Hong.
2027 might bring double-digit wafer price hikes and AI-related revenue at 20-
30% of the mix: During its latest earnings call, UMC guided for a 10% blended
wafer ASP increase in 2027. Given larger exposure to AI server PMIC, industrial, and
automotive, we expect Vanguard to raise 8-inch wafer prices 10-15% for the full year.

Given some customer pushback to front-loaded hikes, the price hike might be split –
twice a year, each 5-10%. Also, the company would still have L TAs expiring in 2027,
so we don’t expect aggressive wafer ASP guidance for 1Q27. With new interposer
production, we believe AI-related revenue could reach 20-30% of total revenue.
Down to UW from EW: The margin impact is larger than we initially expected and
could last 5-10 years.  Our new price target implies 2.8x our 2027e BVPS.

Idea
2
Vanguard could still show strong revenue/margin
performance for 2H26
4Q26 should maintain strong growth: We expect Vanguard to sustain strong
performance into 4Q26, supported by resilient demand from the industrial and
automotive end markets. In addition to a favorable demand outlook, ongoing capacity
optimization initiatives, particularly the transition from thicker-wire to thinner-wire
manufacturing processes, should enhance production efficiency, improve capacity

utilization, and support higher output. These operational improvements, coupled with a
favorable product mix and steady order momentum, could further underpin revenue
growth and profitability in 4Q26. We now expect Vanguard 8-inch process margin to reach
34-36% in both 4Q26 and 1Q27.

Key questions to ask during the upcoming earnings call on November 6:
1.  Price hike into 2027: How much of the incremental cost could Vanguard pass
through  to customers? Could we see double-digit apples-to-apples price hikes into
2027 despite the expiration of some L TAs?

2.  AI-related revenue: How much could  AI related revenue grow in 2027 if we
include both AI server PMIC and interposer production?
3.  Singapore fab – margin dilution and capacity allocation: How could  margin be
affected by VSMC in both 2027 and 2028? How much would depreciation be in
2027 and 2028? How would the company allocate 12-inch capacity between
interposers, NXP, and other customers?
4.  Consumer demand: Has the company seen any slowdown in consumer demand?

And how soon could that capacity be filled up by other sources of demand?
5.  Further capacity expansion: Might the company have further plans to expand 8-
inch capacity or second Singapore fab since the utilization rate has been reaching
>90%?

6
Valuation  methodology
We lower our price target (base case value) to NT$135 from NT$180:  We factor in our
earnings estimate changes for 2027-28 and also lower the intermediate growth rate
assumption from 13% to 10.5% to reflect the long-term margin dilution.
We continue to derive our price target from a residual income model, which we believe
best captures the stock's long-term value. Our key assumptions other than the
intermediate growth rate are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2,

equity risk premium of 6% and risk-free rate of 2%),  and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
We also lower our bull and bear case values from NT$260 and NT$110 to NT$195 and NT
$80, accordingly, implying 4.0x and 1.7x our 2027e BVPS.

Risk Reward - Vanguard International Semiconductor
(5347.TWO)
NT$135.00
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: a cost of equity of 9.2%, a medium-term
growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
Morgan Stanley EstimatesMean
Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$89.00 NT$250.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research
MS Rating
▪ We expect 2026 capex to increase beyond
2025's high level given Singapore fab

expansion, leading to long-term margin
pressure.
▪ We expect PMIC wafer price hikes in the
industry in response to strong demand from
AI. Vanguard could beneﬁt from TSMC
mature node consolidation as well as
interposer production.
▪ The new Singapore fab could cause high-
single-digit margin dilution to the corporate
average in 2027.
▪ VSMC's 12-inch capacity is all fully booked;
production will start to ramp in 2027 with
depreciation pressure during the early stage.

▪ Our UW rating is mostly because of the
margin pressure.

Risk Reward Themes
Disruption: Negative
Pricing Power: Positive
View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$195.00
4.0x base case 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi demand: Auto semi demand is
exceptionally strong; inventory is digested
quickly, and DDI for smartphones and large

panels shows robust growth. We forecast a
>30% revenue CAGR in 2025-28.
Competition from peers eases. We assume:
1) global semi sales growth of >20% and
foundry industry growth of >15% in 2026; 2)
better-than-expected demand for 8"
capacity; and 3) power demand for 5G
tracking ahead of schedule.
NT$135.00
2.8x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth slowdown: Demand has
started to pick up for LDDIC and PMIC,
while auto semis inventory digestion is
reaching an end. We forecast a 25% revenue

CAGR in 2025-28. Vanguard starts to
reverse pricing discounts to customers in
view of stronger demand and some rush
orders in the short run. We assume: 1) global
semi sales show growth in 2026; and 2) the
foundry industry shows single-digit growth
in 2026.
NT$80.00
1.7x base case 2027e BVPS
Semi growth decelerates: Auto semi demand
weakens markedly; power semi inventory
levels remain elevated. Smartphone DDI
shows a worse-than-expected decline.
Intensiﬁed competition and new Chinese

capacity dampen the overall pricing
environment. We forecast <10% revenue
CAGR in 2025-28. We assume: 1) overall
semi industry continues to decelerate in
2026 and foundry growth is stagnant; and 2)
demand for 8" capacity is weaker than
expected owing to faster product migration.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

營收走勢圖

研究員/鄭高輯 OWENCHENG@uni-psg.com

結論: 
力成法說會大致符合預期，其中: (1) 3Q26 毛利率略優於預期，稅後
EPS 符合預期 。(2) 4Q26 營收穩健成長 ，UTR 可望持續提升 。(3) FOPLP
缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響。(4) 2027 年營運將由
FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長。由於營運展望與上季變化不大，
大致維持 2026、2027 年獲利預估，預估稅後 EPS 分別為 12.89 元、
18.57 元，考量力成策略佈局以先進封裝廠為定位，可望擺脫記憶體
景氣循環既定印象，故維持PER 20X，目標價 370 元，維持買進建議。

重點摘要: 
1.3Q26 毛利率略優於預期:  
力成 3Q26 營收+9.6%QoQ(+26.9%YoY)， 主要係AI 伺服器帶旺 eSSD 需
求成長 ， 且先進邏輯封裝 、 子公司超豐， 以及Tera Probe/Tera Power
均有雙位數QoQ 成長表現 。 在毛利率方面 ， 較前季提升2.2pt 至24.0%，
略優於預期的 23.5%，反映產品組合優化，且因應原物料成本提升進
行個位數~雙位數%不等的漲價。OPM 亦較前季提升 2.4pt 至 17.7%，
單季稅後 EPS 3.55 元，大致符合預期的 3.60 元。

2.4Q26 穩健成長，維持 2026 年獲利預估不變:  
展望 4Q26，預估營收持續穩定成長，預期 Dram 產能 UTR 維持高檔且
有價格效益，企業級 SSD 需求也將維持高檔，另 AI 相關應用將持續
扮演主要成長引擎，高階 FCBGA 已成功導入大晶片封裝並延續成長動
能，TPJ+TPW 因伺服器及 AI 強勁需求也將保持穩健，相較之下，超豐
在 AI、HPC 及 IoT 需求成長，但消費性電子 、PC 及 NB 拉貨轉趨保守。
目前整體封裝和測試 UTR 都超過 90%，4Q26 將隨營收增加而持續提
升。預估 2026 全年營收+27.9%YoY，稅後 EPS 12.89 元(原估 12.87
元)。

3. FOPLP 缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響:  
在 FOPLP 進度方面，客戶端產品驗證持續推進，朝向 2027 量產既定
目標穩定推進，且隨著市場需求及量產信心度提升，客戶NPI 的需求
亦顯著增加。在設備交貨狀況方面，儘管半導體廠商對設備需求十分
吃緊，但 在公司與 供應商共同努力下，缺機台情況有明顯改善，
2027~2028 年擴產將按計畫進行，出貨時程不受影響。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

預計 AMD 的 AI 晶片將率先導入 FOPLP， 並於2027 年中以前如期量產，
預估第一階段產能 2K/月。

4.2027 年將由 FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長:  
展望 2027 年，力成營運成長將從過去高度仰賴記憶體產品，重塑為
以先進封裝包括 FOPLP、2.5D、3D、矽光 CPO 及高階 FCBGA 所驅動的
成長模式 。 目前先進封裝佔比30%， 預計2027 年在 FOPLP 設備問題改
善，且如期導入 AMD AI 產品下，先進封裝比重將持續提升。此外，
記憶體供需在 2027 年緩和但仍供不應求，在記憶體大廠產能陸續開
出下，有助於記憶體營收穩增並維持高稼動率。預估 2027 年營收
+22.1%YoY， 稅後淨利+45.8%YoY， 稅後EPS 18.57 元(原估 18.25 元)。

8422 可寧衛(Cleanaway)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：洪  國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 高雄 205 兵工廠專案整治案，己於 2024~2025 年分別認列營
收金額 2 億元與 12 億元，初期主要以開挖為主，掩埋次之，
預期進入中後期掩埋高峰階段，預估 2026~2027 年營收金額
10 億元與 10 億元。  
 小港焚化新爐興建，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，
工程期間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，
毛利率為零 ， 宏遠推估新爐2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元 ，2027
年貢獻營收金額 40 億元。  
一. 預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 0.33 元 
在 BOT 興建工程認列金額較 2Q26 減少因素，可寧衛 3Q26 營收
金額 19.89 億元 ，YoY+88.45% ，QoQ-12.29% ， 預估營業利益5.28
億元， YoY+44.72% ，QoQ-4.47% ，預估稅後盈餘 4.46 億元，
YoY+58.37% ，QoQ-2.79% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.33 元。  
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 0.37 元 
在 4Q26 興建工程進入高峰，預估可寧衛 4Q26 營收金額 22.09
億元， YoY+122.16% ，QoQ+11.04% ，預估營業利益 6.23 億元，
YoY+78.56% ， QoQ+17.95% ，預估稅後盈餘 5.09 億元，
YoY+82.15% ，QoQ+14.12% ，預估稅後 EPS 0.37 元。  
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.25 元 
可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。  
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 1.35 元 
可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。  
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 34 元 
觀察可寧衛歷史 PER11~30X ，2026 年 ROE 16.9% ，PER 21X ，
2027 年 ROE17% ，PER 19.3X 。評價偏低，故投資建議買進，目
標價 34 元（ 25X2027 年稅後 EPS ）。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
可寧衛成立於 1999 年，經營有害事業廢棄物之處理業務，資本額
13.59 億元，主要業務包含有害重金屬污泥廢棄物固化清理、鋼鐵
業集塵灰固化清理、都市垃圾焚化爐飛灰固化清理、有害石綿廢
棄物固化清理與非法棄置場址整治業務，可寧衛為全國最大一站
式開挖、清運、固化、掩埋垂直整合之合法環保公司。可寧衛旗
下有五間子公司，其中岡聯事業以廢棄物清運為主、大倉實業以
一般事業廢棄物掩埋場、可寧衛企業以固化物專屬掩埋場、吉衛
以固化物專屬掩埋場、可寧衛投資主要負責中國大陸業務為主。

圖一、可寧衛集團

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二. 現行廢棄物處理方式與市場概況  
廢棄物主要分成一般廢棄物與事業廢棄物，一般廢棄物主要以家
庭垃圾為主，在經過分類回收後，將進行焚化再中間處理，其中
可寧衛為最終掩埋廠。事業廢棄物在經過資源回收，再區分成有
害廢棄物與一般事業廢棄物，經過焚化處理後，再進行中間處理
（固化處理），最終進入掩埋。可寧衛為最終的固化處理與掩埋。

三.可寧衛公司經營模式與客戶概況  
可寧衛主要客戶分兩大類，第一類為製程客戶，如鋼鐵廠、焚化
爐、電子公司、汙水處理廠等，生產過程中排放出來的廢棄物，
如汞、鋅、銅或其他金屬，首先交由岡聯事業清運，分出一般廢
棄物與有害廢棄，如果是一般廢棄物由大倉事業負責掩埋，若是
有害廢棄物則先做固化處理 ， 再交由可寧衛企業/吉衛來負責掩埋 。

證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

4

第二類客戶為汙染清理客戶，主要處理非法棄置場址與關廠及土
水法列場址，可寧衛歷年來在關廠廠址業務有新竹飛利浦竹北
廠、桃園日商太平洋玻璃廠、台塑前鎮廠、南亞林園 DOP 廠、中
石化前鎮廠、台電興邦獅甲段、台肥前鎮廠、高雄榮民工程等。

圖四、可寧衛服務流程

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

四. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望  
營收來源主要分成製程客戶與汙染清理客戶。在製程客戶營收淡
旺季主要需視客戶淡旺季而定， 1Q 在工作天數與下游客戶出貨量
較少情況下，為傳統淡季，之後逐季走高至 3Q，而後 4Q 將會略
為下滑。在汙染清理客戶方面，主要視案量大小與時間長短而定，
無明顯淡旺季之分。

可寧衛營收結構包括廢棄物固化及開挖、廢棄物掩埋、廢棄物清
運服務、再生紙銷售、預拌混凝土銷售、太陽能發電與其他共七
項。可寧衛 2025 年營收概況，廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金
額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% ；廢棄物掩埋 2025 年營收金額 15.8
億元 ，YoY-10.0% ； 廢棄物清運服務2025 年營收金額 1.44 億元，
YoY+37.1% ；再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.30 億元，
YoY-35.0% ；預拌混凝土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，
YoY+131.6% ；太陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元，
YoY+63.1% 。

廢棄物固化及開挖 2025 年營收金額 17.71 億元， YoY-18.7% 。觀
察營收衰退主因在於高雄中油煉油廠專案整治工程帶動
2023~2024 年廢棄物固化及開挖營收維持在相對高檔水準。 2025
年僅有 205 兵工廠專案，且開挖與固化比重相對較少因素。預期
2026 年，在 205 兵工廠專案整治工程減少，加上國內石綿瓦專案
固化金額未能彌補 205 兵工廠專案 ， 預估廢棄物固化及開挖2026
年營收金額 8.20 億元， YoY-53.7% 。預期 2027 年，在石綿瓦專
案逐漸結束，固化處理量減少，預估廢棄物固化及開挖 2027 年營
收金額 7.50 億元， YoY-8.5% 。

廢棄物掩埋服務 2025 年營收金額 15.8 億元， YoY-10.0% ，觀察
衰退主因在於 2025 年在 205 兵工廠專案，掩埋量相較高雄中油
專案來的低。預期 2026 年，在製程客戶掩埋量小幅增加，但在
205 兵工廠專案掩埋金額小幅增加 ， 預估廢棄物掩埋2026 年營收

續 ， 掩埋量可望微幅增加 ， 預估廢棄物掩埋2027 年營收金額 26.50
億元， YoY+1.90% 。

再生紙銷售主要以上評資源為主。上評資源主要產品為各類牛皮
紙，瓦楞芯紙，工業管用紙，特殊用紙及植生資材之製造及分條
加工。再生紙銷售 2025 年營收金額 4.3 億元， YoY-35.0% 。預估
再生紙銷售 2026 年營收金額 1.7 億元， YoY-60.5% 。預計 4Q26
結束再生紙業務， 2027 年無營收貢獻。

預拌混凝土銷售主要以吉鼎材料為主。吉鼎 CLSM 廠收受再生粒
料，經過混凝土預拌製程，製程控制性低強度回填材料 (CLSM) ，
使用於道路基底層，低密度再生透水混凝土等，將廢棄物產品化，
拓展廢棄物一貫處理流程。吉鼎己於 2Q~3Q24 正式商轉，貢獻
2024 年營收金額 1.03 億元。 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，預拌混凝
土銷售 2025 年營收金額 2.39 億元，YoY+131.6% 。預期 2026 年
第二條產線配合大承再生粒料 4Q26 正式投產，預估預拌混凝土
銷售 2026 年營收金額 3.2 億元， YoY+34.1% 。預期 2027 年在搭
配大承開始投產，帶動預拌混凝土出貨量持續增加，預估預拌混
凝土 2027 年營收金額 4.2 億元， YoY+31.3% 。

太陽能發電主要以大創綠能為主 ， 大創綠能為可寧衛持股 100% 孫
公司。大創綠能主要位於高雄彌陀區設立綠電共生廠，將分三期
規劃，其中第一期裝置容量 69MW ，第二三期裝置容量
53~60MW 。第一期將投入 40 億元建置， 05/2024 開始營運，貢
獻 2024 年營收金額 3.52 億元。在 2025 年貢獻全年度營運，太
陽能發電 2025 年營收金額 5.75 億元， YoY+63.1% 。預期 2026
年，在發電量小幅增加，預估 2026 年太陽能發電營收金額 7.20
億元， YoY+25.3% 。預估 2027 年，在平均單價小幅增加，太陽
能發電營收金額 7.50 億元， YoY+4.20% 。

在其他營收方面， 2026 年將開始貢獻營收，包括高雄 AI 智能高
效焚化爐 BOT 案、大承再生粒料廠。

可寧衛 06/03/2025 公告取得 「高雄市AI 智能高效焚化爐 BOT 案
(小港 )」之最優申請人，可寧衛與台灣人壽成立 SPV 公司，其中
可寧衛持股 55%， 台灣人壽持股45%。 高雄小港焚化爐 ，BOT 案，
舊爐年處理量 34 萬噸，興建新爐年處理量 42 萬噸，營收來源包
括新焚化爐售電收入，新焚化爐代操作與舊焚化爐代操作，其中
新爐將於 2025 年下半年開始興建， 2028~2029 年正式營運，新
爐營運期 20 年。原有焚化爐將有代操作營運，己於 4Q25 正式移
交， 1Q26 開始小幅運作， 2Q26 正式營運，宏遠推估舊爐 2026
年處理量 22~26 萬噸，每噸操作收入 1700~2000 元，宏遠預估小
港焚化爐代操作貢獻 2026 年營收金額 2.5~3 億元（可寧衛持股
55% 進合併營收）。貢獻 2027 年營收金額 3~3.5 億元。

新爐興建方面，在 BOT 會計制度，總興建成本 350 億元，工程期
間 3.5 年，在興建期間需認列工程成本當作營收認列，宏遠推估
新爐 2026 年貢獻營收金額 25 億元， 2027 年貢獻營收金額 40 億
元，上述營收之毛利率為零。

大承再生粒料廠，可寧衛 100% 持股孫公司。大承再生粒料廠主要
使用固化及乾燥製程處理無機污泥 /灰渣 &有機污泥 /無害油泥等製
程再生粒料及資源化燃料，可收受代碼共 35 種（ D 類， R 類， S
類），月處理量 9000 噸，4Q26 末正式營運，預估 2026 年處理量
4.5~6 萬噸，每噸處理費用 6000~8000 元，宏遠預估大承再生粒
料貢獻 2026 年營收金額 0.5~1 億元 。 預估大承再生粒料貢獻2027
年營收金額 5 億元。

可寧衛能源，可寧衛持股 75% 。可寧衛能源主要將在桃園觀音地
區設立固體再生燃料  (SRF) 發電廠，投入 30~35 億元興建， 2022
年底動工。 SRF 發電廠是以回收塑料與廢紙、木材等生質物轉製
成的燃料來發電，再生燃料除了碳排放量大幅低於煤炭外，也能
作為工業鍋爐燃料使用，用途範圍廣泛。可寧衛能源主要營收來
源有兩部分，處理費與售電收入。

可寧衛能源回收塑料與廢紙等生質物，日處理量 350 噸，推估年
處理量 10 萬噸，每噸處理費用 7000 台幣，推估每年營收金額 7
億元。售電收入：再生燃料發電廠裝置容量 17MW ，全年發電度
數 1.2 億度，扣除自用 15% ，每度電 4.74 元計算，年營收金額
4.8 億元 。 宏遠預估2Q~3Q27 正式營運 ， 保守原則先不列入2027
年營收貢獻中。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

預估 2026 年稅後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，稅後 EPS 1.25
元

可寧衛 2026 年雖然在 205 兵工廠整治工程認列金額減少 ， 但2026
年 205 兵工廠營收認列主要以高毛利掩埋比重為主，帶動整體獲
利呈現小幅成長。宏遠預估可寧衛 2026 年營收金額 75.80 億元，
YoY+58.99% ，預估營業利益 20.87 億元， YoY+7.54% ，預估稅
後盈餘 16.95 億元， YoY+16.2% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.25 元。 (以 1
股分割 10 股後股數計算 )

元

可寧衛 2027 年在 205 兵工廠掩埋處理量小幅增加，加上大承再
生粒料廠貢獻全年度營運，預期可寧衛 2027 年營運將持續成長。
宏遠預估可寧衛 2027 年營收金額 96.00 億元， YoY+26.65% ，預
估營業利益 22.97 億元 ，YoY+10.08% ， 預估稅後盈餘18.35 億元 ，
YoY+8.24% ，預估稅後 EPS 1.35 元。 (以 1 股分割 10 股後股數
計算 )

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩, 
4분기까지 지켜보자 
최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

Document 251: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다. 
목표주가 (유지) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/21) 1,417,000원

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Company Brief 
이차전지/석유화학 
Analyst 노우호 
wooho.rho@meritz.co.kr 
RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 510,000원

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

4 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers 
09 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Consumer spending remained very solid in September. Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), or 5.1% excluding gasoline, both the third strongest growth rates over the past four 
years. Month-over-month (MoM) spending was more muted, especially without gas. 
• Spending shows considerable breadth: the vast majority of discretionary retail and services categories are showing growth. 
Moreover, this strength extends across income cohorts, with the ongoing recovery of lower- and middle-income households' 
spending growth. 
• With the winter months fast approaching, holiday spending is already increasing at a solid rate, though it's still early. In Bank of 
America deposit data through September, buy now, pay later (BNPL) usage is rising but not at a rate that is overly concerning. 
However, nearly one in every five households that use BNPL has a repayment of over $500.

Consumer Checkpoint is a regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It aims to provide a holistic and real-time estimate of US 
consumers’ spending and their financial well-being, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America proprietary data. Such data is not 
intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, financial conditions or performance of 
Bank of America. 
A solid September for spending   
Consumer spending continued to look solid in September. Bank of America total aggregated credit and debit card spending per 
household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY), while spending excluding gasoline increased 5.1% YoY. Notably, both are the third 
strongest growth rates in the past four years. Seasonally-adjusted total card spending rose 0.2% month-over-month (MoM), 
while spending excluding gasoline was little changed. Overall, spending growth remains well above the 2025 average rate 
(Exhibit 1).

Most spending categories are growing  
One striking thing about recent card spending, in our view, is that it is well balanced across categories. For example, both 
spending on retail (excluding gasoline) and services grew strongly YoY in September, at 3.5% and 5.4%, respectively. Meanwhile, 
retail spending is showing notably better momentum than in 2025 (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: Spending is rising across retail and services 
Retail (excluding gasoline) and services card spending per household (monthly, seasonally adjusted, index January 2025 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Well balanced across “nice-to-have” and “need-to-have” retail categories  
Is the momentum in retail spending reflective of “nice-to-have” discretionary or “need-to-have” necessity spending? In retail, 
this is a nuanced question, as many categories sit in a gray area between “necessity” and “discretionary”. General merchandise 
stores, for example, sell both essentials like groceries as well as nice-to-have electronics or even jewelry.  
In Exhibit 3, we account for this by grouping retail spending into “more discretionary” (furniture, electronics, building materials, 
online retailers, clothing retailers and miscellaneous stores), “partly discretionary” (auto, health and personal care retailers, 
general merchandise) and “mostly necessity” (gas and groceries). Even within these categories the edges will be blurred, but the 
overall impression is that retail spending growth is widely distributed, with spending rising across all fronts. One caveat: “mostly 
discretionary” spending has been making a smaller contribution since June. 
Exhibit 3: Retail spending is distributed across discretionary and necessity categories  
Percentage points contribution to YoY growth by type of retail spending (three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data. See methodology for category aggregation definitions.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Looking at the share of “more discretionary” retail spending categories that are increasing YoY underscores that growth is well 
balanced and broad-based (Exhibit 4). Non-store and online retailers and miscellaneous stores saw some of the fastest growth, 
while furniture and building materials were among the laggards, though other durable categories showed some strength.

For services, growth is driven mostly by discretionary spending  
In services spending, on the other hand, the boundaries between discretionary and necessity spending are often clearer. Travel 
spending (e.g., airlines, lodging) can be thought of as, by and large, discretionary. At the same time, utility bills, rent and 
insurance payments are necessities.  
And in services, discretionary spending remains the clear driver, accounting for over three-quarters of YoY spending growth in 
September (Exhibit 6). Leisure and housing services are major components of growth, though travel and restaurants also made 
positive contributions. However, as we noted last month strength in airline spending largely reflects price rises rather than 
transactions growth (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Still sizzling).  
Exhibit 6: Services spending growth has been mostly driven by discretionary spending over the past year

Lower-income households’ spending outpaced all but the top 5% 
The breadth in spending growth across categories is also evident across income cohorts. In September, lower-income 
households’ total card spending growth was 5.5% YoY, while the equivalent figures for middle- and higher-income households 
were 4.9% and 4.8% YoY, respectively. The top 5% of households by income continue to show the fastest spending growth, 
5.9% in September, though this has eased somewhat (Exhibit 7). 
Overall, the divergences across income cohorts are much smaller than they were in 2025 and the first three months of this year. 
Just focusing on spending excluding key necessities (gasoline, food and utilities) doesn’t meaningfully change the picture of 
convergence across income cohorts (Exhibit 8).  
Exhibit 7: The top 5% saw the strongest spending growth of any 
income cohort… 
Total card spending per household by income tercile and the top 5% 
(three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 8: …though discretionary spending for the top 5% has eased 
some since June 
Discretionary card spending per household by income tercile and the top 
5% (three-month moving average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The strength in lower- and middle-income households’ discretionary spending is occurring despite necessity spending shifting 
from a tailwind to a headwind over the past year amid rising gas prices (Exhibit 9). In our view, a key component of why these 
households have been able to combine rising necessity spending with strength in discretionary is the labor market and a 
recovery in their after-tax wage growth (Exhibit 10).  
In September, lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY from 4.7% YoY in August, while growth for middle- and higher-
income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. These changes were modest, and lower-income households are 
continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” pattern we 
observed over much of 2025 and 2026.  
Exhibit 9: Necessity spending has gone from a tailwind to a

Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories 
Share of spending by the top 5% of households by income across select discretionary categories (three-month moving average to September 2026, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.  
Exhibit 12: It’s early days, but holiday spending is strong so far this year

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.
